The Houthis in turn accused the Saleh government of being backed by Saudi Arabia and of using [Al-Qaeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula "Al-Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula") to repress them.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-201) Under the next President Hadi, Gulf Arab states accused Iran of backing the Houthis financially and militarily, though Iran denied this, and they were themselves backers of President Hadi.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-BBC22032022-202) Despite confirming statements by Iranian and Yemeni officials in regards to Iranian support in the form of trainers, weaponry, and money, the Houthis denied reception of substantial financial or arm support from Iran.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Iran_support-98)[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-203) [Joost Hiltermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joost_Hiltermann "Joost Hiltermann") of _[Foreign Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Policy "Foreign Policy")_ wrote that whatever little material support the Houthis may have received from Iran, the intelligence and military support by US and UK for the [Saudi Arabian-led coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen "Saudi Arabian-led intervention in Yemen") exceed that by many factors.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Foreign-Policy-2017-204)

In April 2015, the [United States National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_National_Security_Council "United States National Security Council") spokesperson [Bernadette Meehan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernadette_Meehan "Bernadette Meehan") remarked that "It remains our assessment that Iran does not exert command and control over the Houthis in Yemen".[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-205) [Joost Hiltermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joost_Hiltermann "Joost Hiltermann") wrote that Iran does not control the Houthis' decision-making as evidenced by Houthis' flat rejection of Iran's demand not to take over Sanaa in 2015.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Foreign-Policy-2017-204) Thomas Juneau, writing in the journal, _[International Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Affairs_(journal) "International Affairs (journal)")_, states that even though Iran's support for Houthis has increased since 2014, it remains far too limited to have a significant impact in the balance of power in Yemen.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-JUNEAU2-42) The [Quincy Institute for Responsible Statecraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Institute_for_Responsible_Statecraft "Quincy Institute for Responsible Statecraft") argues that Teheran's influence over the movement has been "greatly exaggerated" by "the Saudis, their [coalition partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen "Saudi Arabian-led intervention in Yemen") (mainly the United Arab Emirates), and their \[lobbyists\] in Washington."[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-206) Similarly, academics such as Marieke Brandt and Charles Schmitz have stated that the allegation that the Houthis are merely an Iranian proxy force has its roots in political narratives by Saleh, Saudi Arabia, the United States and other anti-Houthi forces.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-schmitz-104)[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-brandt-100):146,204–205 While the Houthis have praised post- [Islamic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Revolution "Iranian Revolution") Iran for its opposition to American and Israeli [imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperialism "Imperialism") in the Middle East, they have also criticized Iranian political and religious doctrine, including Iran's state religion of [Twelver Shi'ism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelver_Shi'ism "Twelver Shi'ism").[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-schmitz-104)

A December 2009 cable between Sanaa and various intelligence agencies in the [US diplomatic cables leak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_diplomatic_cables_leak "United States diplomatic cables leak") states that US State Dept. analysts believed the Houthis obtained weapons from the Yemeni [black market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_market "Black market") and corrupt members of the Yemenis Republican Guard.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-207) On the edition of 8 April 2015 of _[PBS Newshour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS_Newshour "PBS Newshour")_, [Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State "United States Secretary of State") John Kerry stated that the US knew Iran was providing military support to the Houthi rebels in Yemen, adding that Washington "is not going to stand by while the region is destabilised".[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-208)

Phillip Smyth of the [Washington Institute for Near East Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Institute_for_Near_East_Policy "Washington Institute for Near East Policy") told [Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider "Business Insider") that Iran views Shia groups in the Middle East as "integral elements to the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC)". Smyth claimed that there is a strong bond between Iran and the Houthi uprising working to overthrow the government in Yemen. According to Smyth, in many cases Houthi leaders go to Iran for ideological and religious education, and Iranian and Hezbollah leaders have been spotted on the ground advising the Houthi troops, and these Iranian advisers are likely responsible for training the Houthis to use the type of sophisticated guided missiles fired at the US Navy.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-209)

To some commentators (e.g., Alex Lockie of _[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider "Business Insider")_), Iran's support for the revolt in Yemen is "a good way to bleed the Saudis", a recognized regional and ideological rival of Iran. Essentially, from that perspective, Iran is backing the Houthis to fight against a Saudi-led coalition of Gulf States whose aim is to maintain control of Yemen.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-BI_Lockie20161013-200)

In early 2013, photographs released by the Yemeni government show the United States Navy and Yemen's security forces seizing a class of "either modern Chinese- or Iranian-made" shoulder-fired, heat-seeking anti-aircraft missiles "in their standard packaging", missiles "not publicly known to have been out of state control", raising concerns of Iran's arming of the rebels.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-210) In April 2016, the U.S. Navy intercepted a large Iranian arms shipment, seizing thousands of AK-47 rifles, rocket-propelled grenade launchers, and 0.50-caliber machine guns, a shipment described as likely headed to Yemen by the Pentagon.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-211)[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Tomlinsom_Fox20160404-212) Based on 2019 reporting from _[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post "The Jerusalem Post")_, the Houthis have also repeatedly used a drone nearly identical to [Iran Aircraft Manufacturing Industrial Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Aircraft_Manufacturing_Industrial_Company "Iran Aircraft Manufacturing Industrial Company")'s [Ababil-T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HESA_Ababil#Twin-tail_variant "HESA Ababil") drone in strikes against Saudi Arabia.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-213) In late October 2023, Israel stated that it had intercepted a "surface-to-surface long-range ballistic missile and two cruise missiles that were fired by the Houthi rebels in Yemen"; per reporting from _[Axios.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axios.com "Axios.com")_, this "was Israel's first-ever operational use of the [Arrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrow_(missile_family) "Arrow (missile family)") system for intercepting ballistic missiles since the war began".[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-214)

The continuing interceptions and seizures of weapons at sea, attributed to Iranian origins, is a matter tracked by the [United States Institute of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Institute_of_Peace "United States Institute of Peace").[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-215)

### Iran

In 2013, an Iranian vessel was seized and discovered to be carrying [Katyusha rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katyusha_rockets "Katyusha rockets"), heat-seeking [surface-to-air missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surface-to-air_missiles "Surface-to-air missiles"), RPG-7s, Iranian-made night vision goggles and artillery systems that track land and navy targets 40km away. That was en route to the Houthis.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-216)

In March 2017, [Qasem Soleimani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qasem_Soleimani "Qasem Soleimani"), head of Iran's [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds_Force "Quds Force"), met with Iran's [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") (IRGC) regarding the "newly accelerated effort to empower the Houthis", including the provision of weapons and training.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-217) Soleimani was quoted as saying, "At this meeting, they agreed to increase the amount of help, through training, arms and financial support."\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Despite the Iranian government, and Houthis both officially denying Iranian support for the group. Brigadier General [Ahmad Asiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Asiri_(general) "Ahmad Asiri (general)"), the spokesman of the Saudi-led coalition told [Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters") that evidence of Iranian support was manifested in the Houthi use of [Kornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kornet "Kornet") [anti-tank guided missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-tank_guided_missiles "Anti-tank guided missiles") which had never been in use with the [Yemeni military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Yemen_Armed_Forces "Republic of Yemen Armed Forces") or with the Houthis and that the arrival of Kornet missiles had only come at a later time.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-218) In the same month the IRGC had altered the routes used in transporting equipment to the Houthis by spreading out shipments to smaller vessels in Kuwaiti territorial waters in order to avoid naval patrols in the [Gulf of Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Oman "Gulf of Oman") due to sanctions imposed, shipments reportedly included parts of missiles, launchers, and drugs.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-219)

In May 2018, the United States imposed sanctions on Iran's IRGC, which was also listed as a [designated terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_designated_terrorist_groups "List of designated terrorist groups") by the US over its role in providing support for the Houthis, including help with manufacturing [ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic_missiles "Ballistic missiles") used in attacks targeting cities and oil fields in Saudi Arabia.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-220) In August 2018, despite previous Iranian denial of military support for the Houthis, IRGC commander [Nasser Shabani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Shabani "Nasser Shabani") was quoted by the Iranian [Fars News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fars_News_Agency "Fars News Agency") as saying, "We (IRGC) told Yemenis \[Houthi rebels\] to strike two Saudi oil tankers, and they did it", on 7 August 2018. In response to Shabani's account, the IRGC released a statement saying that the quote was a "Western lie" and that Shabani was a retired commander, despite no actual reports of his retirement after 37 years in the IRGC, and media linked to the Iranian government confirming he was still enlisted with the IRGC.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-221) Furthermore, while the Houthis and the Iranian government have previously denied any military affiliation,[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Iran_support-98) Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Khamenei "Ali Khamenei") announced his "spiritual" support of the movement in a personal meeting with the Houthi spokesperson Mohammed Abdul Salam in [Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran "Tehran"), in the midst of ongoing conflicts in Aden in 2019.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-222)[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-223)

In 2024, commanders from IRGC and Hezbollah were reported to be actively involved on the ground in Yemen, overseeing and directing [Houthi attacks on Red Sea shipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_involvement_in_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war "Houthi involvement in the Israel–Hamas war"), according to a report by Reuters.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-hez-224) In July 2024, the United States targeted new sanctions focusing on IRGC ties with the group. The Houthis dismissed the sanctions as pathetic and powerless.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-225) In 2024, Israel also placed sanctions on the Houthis.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-226)

In September 2026, the [G7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G7 "G7") foreign ministers issued a joint statement condemning Houthi attacks on Saudi Arabia and calling on Iran to end its support for the Houthis.[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-227)

### North Korea

In August 2018, [Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters") reported that a confidential United Nations investigation had found the North Korean government had failed to discontinue its nuclear and missile delivery programs, and in conjunction, was "cooperating militarily with Syria" and was "trying to sell weapons to Yemen's Houthis".[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-North_Korean_aid-228)[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-North_Korean_weapons_aid_1-229)[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-North_Korean_weapons_aid_2-230)

In August 2019, the South Korean [National Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Intelligence_Service_(South_Korea) "National Intelligence Service (South Korea)") had tracked the Scuds missiles (used to attack Saudi Arabia) back to North Korea.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-231)

In January 2024, South Korea's [Yonhap News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonhap_News_Agency "Yonhap News Agency") reported that North Korea had evidently shipped weapons to Houthis via Iran, based on the writings in [Hangul script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hangul "Hangul") that were found on missiles launched towards Israel.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-232)

North Korea considers the Houthis as a "resistance force".[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-233)[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-234)

In March 2025, after the [airstrikes by the US Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_attacks_in_Yemen_(March_2025%E2%80%93present) "United States attacks in Yemen (March 2025–present)") commenced, North Korean Ambassador to Egypt, Ma Dong-hee, who is also accredited to Yemen, condemned the attacks on the Houthis as a threat to regional and global order.[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-235)[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-236)

### Russia

It was noted by _[Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek "Newsweek")_ in July 2024 that the Houthis were in possession of Russian-made [P-800 Oniks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P-800_Oniks "P-800 Oniks") missiles, and that the transfer had likely occurred via Syria and Iran.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Russia-237) In July 2024, _[The Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal")_ reported that US officials saw increasing indications that Russia was considering arming the Houthis with advanced anti-ship missiles via Iranian smuggling routes in response to [US support for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "United States and the Russian invasion of Ukraine") during [Russia's invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Russian invasion of Ukraine").[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-toi1-238) However, it did not follow through due to pushback by the US and Saudi Arabia.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-cnn1-239) In August 2024, _[Middle East Eye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Eye "Middle East Eye")_, citing a US official, reported that personnel of Russia's [GRU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GRU_(Russian_Federation) "GRU (Russian Federation)") were stationed in Houthi-controlled parts of Yemen to assist the militia's attacks on merchant ships.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Mathews2024-240) In October, _The Wall Street Journal_ reported that Russia was supplying the Houthis with [geospatial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geospatial_intelligence "Geospatial intelligence") to target Western ships.[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-241)

### China

Main article: [China–Yemen relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Yemen_relations "China–Yemen relations")

China recognizes the internationally recognized government of Yemen, currently the [Presidential Leadership Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Leadership_Council "Presidential Leadership Council").[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Habtoor1-242) Chinese diplomatic discourses generally avoid criticizing the Houthis by name.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:13-243):164 China typically makes its criticisms of the Houthi movement via indirect statements, which condemn actions without specifically naming the party responsible.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:13-243):164 During the [Houthi attacks on commercial vessels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_attacks_on_commercial_vessels "Houthi attacks on commercial vessels") in response to the [Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war"), China stated that the Houthis should respect the navigation rights of the merchant ships of all countries in the Red Sea in accordance with international law and "immediately cease their acts of harassment."[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:13-243):141 China requested that [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Iran_relations "China–Iran relations") use its influence with the Houthi movement in attempt to restrain Houthi attacks on civilian ships.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:13-243):141

Two China based companies were sanctioned by America in 2024 for providing "dual-use materials and components needed to manufacture, maintain, and deploy an arsenal of advanced missiles and unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) against U.S. and allied interests."[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-244) A report by the [Foundation for Defense of Democracies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_Defense_of_Democracies "Foundation for Defense of Democracies") stated that the Houthis were using weapons made in China for their attacks on shipping in the Red Sea in exchange for Chinese ships having safe passage through the Sea. Another report from Israel's i24 News stated that China provided the Houthis with "advanced components and guidance equipment" for their missiles.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-China-245) The [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War "Institute for the Study of War") reported that the Houthis supplement their weaponry through additional arms and dual use components sourced from Russia or China. For example, Yemeni border customs seized 800 Chinese-made drone propellers in a shipment bound for the Houthis, and in August 2024 had also purchased Hydrogen Fuel Cylinders from Chinese suppliers which aimed to increase the range and payloads of the Houthis' drones.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-246) According to _The Wall Street Journal_, the Houthis sent a group from [Saada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saada "Saada") to Beijing to study Mandarin and manage the supply of drones and missile guidance systems from China and Hong Kong to Yemen.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:1-247)

According to the [United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State "United States Department of State"), Chinese state-owned [Chang Guang Satellite Technology Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang_Guang_Satellite_Technology_Corporation "Chang Guang Satellite Technology Corporation") has provided [geospatial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geospatial_intelligence "Geospatial intelligence") to the Houthis to target U.S. warships in the Red Sea.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:1-247)[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-248)[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-249) The reason for the company supplying such satellite imagery was speculated by academic Thomas Juneau to be for China to ensure its commercial ships in the Red Sea are not targeted.[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-250) On 28 July 2026, following the [Houthi blockade of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_blockade_of_Saudi_Arabia "Houthi blockade of Saudi Arabia") that started on 20 July, _[Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters")_ reported that China had held direct talks with the Houthis and Iran to enable its tankers to pass through the Red Sea without being attacked. It reported that China was clearing each vessel with the Houthis individually, and both sides have informed Iran of their actions.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-251) On 17 September, following the [2026 Yemen offensives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Yemen_offensives "2026 Yemen offensives"), _Reuters_ reported that China was seeking to pressure Iran to reign in the Houthis and help prevent the conflict spreading further across energy routes on which China depends. It reporters that Iran responded by saying that stability and peace in the region depend on ending the Iran war.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-252) _The Wall Street Journal_ reported in October 2026 that Houthis have relied on Chinese equipment and parts to build a version of the [Kheibar Shekan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kheibar_Shekan "Kheibar Shekan") ballistic missile.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-253) Shao Zheng, chargé d'affaires of the Chinese Embassy to Yemen, denied the reports as baseless and urged the Houthis to abandon military action and return to peaceful dialogue.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Habtoor1-242)

### Salafi Jihadist groups

In 2024, a UN report states that [Al-Shabaab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shabaab_(militant_group) "Al-Shabaab (militant group)") and Houthis had a relationship that was "transactional or opportunistic, and not ideological", while a 2025 report states that their relationship was deepening and posed a threat to regional security.[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-254) According to the [Africa Center for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa_Center_for_Strategic_Studies "Africa Center for Strategic Studies"), al Shabaab is provided with weapons and training while also receiving assistance in expanding the criminal enterprises that fund their operations. While the Houthis benefit by expanding their influence, strengthening Anti-American forces in the region, and weakening pro-American forces in the region. Additionally both sides assist each other in smuggling operations.[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-africacenter.org-255)

The [Al-Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula "Al-Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula") (AQAP) and Houthis had previously fought, but since 2022 they have had a ceasefire. As part of this ceasefire they have cooperated in attacks against Yemeni government, provided safe havens for each other in their territories, and cooperating in security and intelligence.[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-africacenter.org-255)

## Human rights violations

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Anti-Houthi_protests_in_Sana%27a_%282_December_2017%29.png/250px-Anti-Houthi_protests_in_Sana%27a_%282_December_2017%29.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Anti-Houthi_protests_in_Sana'a_(2_December_2017).png) Protestors tear down a Houthi poster amid the [battle of Sanaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sanaa_(2017) "Battle of Sanaa (2017)") between pro-Houthi forces, and forces loyal to ex-President Ali Abdullah Saleh.

According to the Panel of Experts on Yemen established pursuant to Security Council resolution 2140, the Houthis have carried out a wide range of human rights violations, including violations of international humanitarian law and abuse of women and children. Children as young as 13 have been arrested for "indecent acts" for alleged homosexual orientation or "political cases" when their families do not comply with Houthi ideology or regulations. Minors share cells with adult prisoners, and according to unspecified reports that the Panel has deemed "credible", boys held in Al-Shahid Al-Ahmar police station in Sana'a are systematically raped.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-S/2023/833-256)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-S/2022/50-257) Aside from the Panel of Experts, London-based Arabic newspaper _[Asharq Al-Awsat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asharq_Al-Awsat "Asharq Al-Awsat")_ alleges that the Houthis have revived [slavery in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_Yemen "Slavery in Yemen").[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-258)

### Child soldiers and human shields

Houthis have been accused of violations of international humanitarian law, such as using [child soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_soldiers "Child soldiers"),[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-259)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-CC18-260)[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-HRW070415-33) shelling civilian areas,[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Al-HajYoussef-31) forced evacuations and executions.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-261) According to [Human Rights Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch "Human Rights Watch"), Houthis intensified their recruitment of children in 2015. UNICEF mentioned that children with the Houthis and other armed groups in Yemen comprise up to a third of all fighters in Yemen.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-HRW12052015-147) Human Rights Watch has further accused Houthi forces of using landmines in Yemen's third-largest city of Taizz, which has caused many civilian casualties and prevented the return of families displaced by the fighting.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-262) HRW has also accused the Houthis of interfering with the work of Yemen's human rights advocates and organizations.[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-263)

In 2009, HRW researcher Christoph Wilcke said that although the Republic of Yemen Government accused the Houthis of using civilians as human shields, HRW did not have enough evidence to conclude that the Houthis were intentionally doing so. Nonetheless, Wilcke stated that there may have been cases in which HRW was unable to document.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-scoop-158) Akram Al Walidi, one of four journalists detained by the Houthis on spying charges[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-264) and then released in April 2023 as part of a prisoner exchange deal between the former and the internationally recognized government of Yemen, said he felt like the four were human shields after the Houthis moved them to one of their military camps at [Sanaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanaa "Sanaa") in October 2020 since it was an expected target of Saudi airstrikes.[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-265)

### Hostage-taking

According to the Human Rights Watch, the Houthis also use hostage taking as a tactic to generate profit. Human Rights Watch documented 16 cases in which Houthi authorities held people unlawfully, in large part to extort money from relatives or to exchange them for people held by opposing forces.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-266)

### Diversion of international aid

The [United Nations World Food Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Food_Programme "World Food Programme") has accused the Houthis of diverting food aid and illegally removing food lorries from distribution areas, with rations sold on the open market or given to those not entitled to them.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-267) The WFP has also warned that aid could be suspended to areas of Yemen under the control of Houthi rebels due to "obstructive and uncooperative" Houthi leaders that have hampered the independent selection of beneficiaries.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-268) WFP spokesman Herve Verhoosel stated "The continued blocking by some within the Houthi leadership of the biometric registration ... is undermining an essential process that would allow us to independently verify that food is reaching ... people on the brink of famine". The WFP has warned that "unless progress is made on previous agreements we will have to implement a phased suspension of aid". The [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council "Norwegian Refugee Council") has stated that they share the WPF's frustrations and reiterate to the Houthis to allow humanitarian agencies to distribute food.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-269)[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-270)

### Abuse of women and girls

The [United Nations Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Council "United Nations Human Rights Council") published a report covering the period July 2019 to June 2020, which contained evidence of the Houthis' recruitment of boys as young as seven years old and the recruitment of 34 girls aged between 13 and 17 years of age, to act as spies, recruiters of other children, guards, medics, and members of a female fighting force. Twelve girls suffered [sexual violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_violence "Sexual violence"), [arranged marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arranged_marriages "Arranged marriages"), and [child marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_marriage "Child marriage") as a result of their recruitment.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-271)

Under Houthi-controlled areas, women have been blocked from travelling without a _mahram_ (male guardian) even for essential healthcare. This also affected humanitarian operations by the United Nations in Yemen, forcing female staff to office jobs. The Houthis use allegations of prostitution as a tool for public defamation against Yemeni women, including those in the diaspora engaged in politics, civil society, or human rights activism, alongside threats to individuals and families. Detained women have been sexually assaulted, subjected to virginity tests, and often blocked from accessing essential goods. Torture of female detainees is also carried out by the _Zaynabiyat_, the Houthi female police wing.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-S/2023/833-256)[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-272)[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-273)[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-274)

[Anadolu Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anadolu_Agency "Anadolu Agency") reported on Yemen-based rights groups documenting 1,181 violations against women committed by Houthis from 2017 to 2020.[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-275) Yemeni activist Samira Abdullah al-Houry was held in a Houthi jail for three months and gave numerous interviews after her release on alleged torture and rape by Houthi guards.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-276) Her testimony contributed to [UN Security Council sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2564 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 2564") being imposed on two Houthi security officials in February 2021. It was later alleged that she admitted some of her testimony was untrue, and she had embellished claims at the request of Saudi officials.[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-277)

### Abuse of LGBTQ+ people

According to [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International") on 9 February 2024, two Houthi-run courts in Yemen sentenced 48 individuals either to death, flogging, or prison over charges related to same-sex conduct in the past month.[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-278)

### Abuse of migrants

According to Human Rights Watch, Houthi militias have "beaten, raped, and tortured detained migrants and asylum seekers from the Horn of Africa."[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-279) UN experts have warned that female migrants face sexual violence, forced labor, and forced drug trafficking by smugglers who collaborate with the Houthi-controlled Yemen Immigration, Passport and Nationality Authority (IPNA).[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-280)

### Detention of UN worker

Mohammad el Shami, a worker at the UN Human Rights Office in Yemen, died in October 2026, after being held in Houthi custody since June 2024. UN High Commissioner for Human Rights [Volker Türk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volker_T%C3%BCrk "Volker Türk") called for an investigation into the death.[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-281)

## Governance

Main articles: [Supreme Revolutionary Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Revolutionary_Committee "Supreme Revolutionary Committee"), [Supreme Political Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Political_Council "Supreme Political Council"), and [Houthi-controlled Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi-controlled_Yemen "Houthi-controlled Yemen")

According to a 2009 [leaked US Embassy cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_diplomatic_cables_leak "United States diplomatic cables leak"), Houthis have reportedly established courts and prisons in areas they control. They impose their own laws on local residents, demand protection money, and dispense rough justice by ordering executions. [AP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associated_Press "Associated Press")'s reporter, Ahmad al-Haj argued that the Houthis were winning hearts and minds by providing security in areas long neglected by the Yemeni government while limiting the arbitrary and abusive power of influential sheikhs. According to the Civic Democratic Foundation, Houthis help resolve conflicts between tribes and reduce the number of revenge killings in areas they control. The US ambassador believed that the reports that explain Houthi role as arbitrating local disputes were likely.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-scoop-158)

### Public opinion

A survey conducted in 2024 by the _[Sana'a Center for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sana'a_Center_for_Strategic_Studies "Sana'a Center for Strategic Studies")_ found that only 8% of Yemenis in Houthi-controlled areas had a positive view of the Houthi movement, compared to 3% in both government-controlled areas and contested areas. Conversely, 20%, 34%, and 39% in these areas, respectively, expressed negative views.[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-282)

## Sanctions

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Countries_designating_the_Houthis_as_terrorist.svg/250px-Countries_designating_the_Houthis_as_terrorist.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Countries_designating_the_Houthis_as_terrorist.svg)

Yemen

Designated the Houthis as a terrorist organization

In January 2024, the US and UK imposed sanctions on key Houthi figures, including the defense minister, in response to the Houthi attacks on international shipping in the Red Sea that escalated in November 2023. The new sanctions were imposed in addition to the existing sanctions against 11 Houthi individuals and 2 entities, which remained in force.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-283)

On 28 April 2025, the [U.S. Treasury Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Treasury_Department "U.S. Treasury Department") sanctioned three shipping companies for their role in delivering oil products to the Houthis. The deliveries took place via the Houthi-controlled port of [Ras Isa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ras_Isa?action=edit&redlink=1 "Ras Isa (page does not exist)").[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Reuters3companies-284)

## United States attacks on Yemen

Further information: [March–May 2025 United States attacks in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March%E2%80%93May_2025_United_States_attacks_in_Yemen "March–May 2025 United States attacks in Yemen")

The U.S. launched a [military campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March%E2%80%93April_2025_United_States_attacks_in_Yemen "March–April 2025 United States attacks in Yemen") against Yemen in mid-March 2025, which it said was directed at Houthi military and strategic targets. The attacks killed 153 civilians and wounded 243 more, according to the [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon "The Pentagon")'s own assessment.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-285) The deaths included women and children, according to the Houthis. The group, backed by Iran,[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-cfr240325-29)[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-286)[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-287) state that their operations, which have affected global trade, are in solidarity with Palestinians in Gaza.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-:2-288)[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-289)[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-290)

## Notes

1. [&#8202;](#refnote-twelver)[Jump up to: 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_ref-twelver_15-0) [2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_ref-twelver_15-1) [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_ref-twelver_15-2)The Houthis have been accused, even by a number of fellow Zaydis, of secretly being converts or followers of the [Twelver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelver "Twelver") branch, which is the official religion of [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran").[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Al-Shamahi-101)[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-Profile:_Who_are_Yemen's_Houthis-102)[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-103) However, this claim is tied to efforts by the [Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Abdullah_Saleh "Ali Abdullah Saleh") regime and subsequent Yemeni governments to portray the Houthis as a force directly controlled by Iran,[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-brandt-100):204–205 and is not backed up by evidence.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-schmitz-104)[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_note-105)
2. [&#8202;](#refnote-24)[↑](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis#cite_ref-24 "Jump up")[/ˈhuːθiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English"); [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): الْحُوثِيّوُن, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic "Romanization of Arabic"):_al-Ḥūthiyūn_, ar
3.
